FAILURE SIGNALS stay fail-closed: a failure is non-blocking ONLY when it is provably attributable — bound to a projection scenario outside the change scope, OR carrying an ID declared as a scenario inside a SIBLING active change's cleanly-parsed delta (strict parse, zero problems, >0 operations; only ADDED/MODIFIED block bodies count — a parallel change's own red is that change's business; the sibling scan is part of the same single title batch; malformed, escaping, symlinked or unreadable sibling material grants NO exemption — skipping is the fail-closed direction; broken sibling material never INDEPENDENTLY creates an infra ERROR, and any failure left unattributed still blocks as GAPS); every `unattributedFailures` entry (no ID, no provenance — it may be this change's own test missing its ID) and every FAILING orphan whose ID appears in NEITHER the projection NOR any sibling delta blocks the change verdict exactly as today. The store report is a COMPLETE informative evaluation of the whole projection against the same TAP snapshot: `boundRed`, `unbound`, true-`orphan`, `unidentified`, `unattributedFailures`, `duplicates` (each a full list whose length is its count; `boundGreen` as a bare count) — out-of-scope reds, duplicates and unidentified MUST stay visible there. Execution follows a conditional single-run contract: invalid pattern → 0 projection builds / 0 content reads / 0 test spawns / 0 TAP parses (the fail-early order stands); projection or title-batch matcher failure → exactly 1 projection, 0 spawns; the normal and post-TAP-error paths → exactly 1 projection, 1 spawn, 1 parse; both views share the one projection and the one parsed TAP snapshot — the report never re-runs tests. Exit semantics: infra ERROR classes are unchanged; solely for `--change`, a non-zero test-process status that is EXPLAINED by parsed failures (`failCount > 0`) with no infra error no longer forces a non-zero exit — a clean change verdict is GREEN exit 0 (a scoped amendment to the non-zero rule; an unexplained non-zero stays ERROR exit 2). `--specs` runs are byte-identical to before.
   250	
   251	#### Scenario: SR-56 the change verdict judges only the change scope
   252	- WHEN the store has `R-A`(XA-01) and `R-B`(XB-01), the delta ADDs `R-C`(XC-01), and the test command prints `ok XC-01`, `ok XA-01`, `ok XZ-99` (a PASSING orphan), `not ok XA-01 again` (an out-of-scope bound red), then exits 1
   253	- THEN `verify --change` is GREEN exit 0; the store report carries unbound=[XB-01], orphan=[XZ-99], boundRed=[XA-01]; and the same fixture through `--specs apriori/specs` behaves exactly as today (GAPS). Conversely an ID-less `not ok`, or a FAILING orphan whose ID no sibling change declares (`not ok XQ-77`), turns the change verdict GAPS — unprovable failures stay blocking; a failing test whose ID is declared in a sibling active change's delta is that sibling's business and does not block
   254	
   255	#### Scenario: SR-57 change gaps still fail
   256	- WHEN XC-01 has no test (and separately: its only test is `not ok`)
   257	- THEN the run is GAPS exit 1 with the change verdict carrying unbound=[XC-01] (respectively boundRed=[XC-01])
   258	
   259	#### Scenario: SR-58 operation semantics bound the scope
   260	- WHEN a delta MODIFIES a block to contain XA-01 and XA-02, REMOVEs a block whose scenario still has a lingering test, RENAMEs `R-A -> R-G` (block contains XA-01), and separately renames-then-modifies (`RENAMED R-A -> R-G` + `MODIFIED R-G`) and renames-then-removes
   261	- THEN the modified block's XA-01 and XA-02 are both in scope; the removed block's scenario is not demanded and its lingering test lands in the store report's orphan; the renamed block's XA-01 stays in scope under its unchanged ID; the rename-then-modify final block is in scope with `operations: ["RENAMED","MODIFIED"]`; the rename-then-remove final deprecated block is out of scope
   262	
   263	#### Scenario: SR-59 in-scope strictness blocks the verdict
   264	- WHEN a scoped block contains an unidentified scenario, or a scoped scenario ID also occurs in an untouched store block
   265	- THEN the change verdict is not GREEN (scoped unidentified and cross-boundary duplicates count); a duplicate entirely outside the scope leaves the verdict GREEN-able and appears in the store report's duplicates
   266	
   267	#### Scenario: SR-60 the store report loses nothing
   268	- WHEN out-of-scope tests are red, an out-of-scope duplicate exists, and an out-of-scope block carries an unidentified scenario
   269	- THEN none of them blocks the change verdict, and every one of them appears in the store report (boundRed / duplicates / unidentified respectively)
   270	
   271	#### Scenario: SR-61 explained non-zero status can be GREEN, unexplained stays ERROR
   272	- WHEN the test command exits 1 and every parsed failure is bound to an out-of-scope projection scenario (known out-of-scope red) and the change verdict is clean
   273	- THEN the run is GREEN exit 0; WHEN the only failures are unattributed or failing orphans THEN the run is GAPS (they block, per the fail-closed rule); and WHEN the command exits non-zero with `failCount === 0` THEN the run stays ERROR exit 2 (the unexplained-non-zero rule is untouched)
   274	
   275	#### Scenario: SR-62 the zero-scope truth table holds
   276	- WHEN the change scope is empty
   277	- THEN a pure-REMOVED delta over a non-empty projection is GREEN with the note `0 scenario(s) in change scope (removal-only change)`; a mixed/empty-block delta (ADDED/MODIFIED/RENAMED yielding no scenarios) is GREEN with the generic note plus an ops summary and never the removal-only wording; an all-empty projection is ERROR exit 2 (the global vacuous rule); a scope whose only occurrences are unidentified is GAPS exit 1; a zero-op or malformed delta keeps today's projection-failure ERROR
   278	
   279	#### Scenario: SR-63 one projection, one test run, one parse
   280	- WHEN a config-origin `--change` run completes with a counting child-runner seam and a sentinel test command
   281	- THEN the matcher child ran EXACTLY twice — first the full projection-title batch (once, never re-run for the scoped view), second the TAP-description batch — the sentinel counted exactly one test spawn, exactly one projection was built, and both views agree on the same TAP snapshot; a title-batch failure spawns no test (0 spawn), a TAP-batch failure comes after exactly 1 spawn; the invalid-pattern and projection-failure paths spawn nothing (existing assertions)
   282	
   283	#### Scenario: SR-64 the JSON contract is pinned for all four outcome classes
   284	- WHEN `--change --json` runs end GREEN, GAPS, pre-test ERROR, and post-TAP ERROR
   285	- THEN GREEN/GAPS carry top-level change-scoped `clean/result/boundGreen/boundRed/unbound/orphan/unidentified/unattributedFailures/duplicates` plus `storeReport` (six classes; array length = count; `boundGreen` a bare count; `unattributedFailures` keeps `{count, lines}`) and `changeScope` (`requirements` as sorted `{file, name, operations[]}`, `scenarioIds` sorted); BOTH ERROR classes omit `storeReport` and `changeScope` entirely (absent, never null); `projection` keeps its existing shape; a `--specs --json` run is unchanged and never carries either field — proven byte-level: golden captures of `--specs` human and JSON outputs (GREEN/GAPS/ERROR, duplicates/unidentified/unattributed/stderr classes) taken against state A compare byte-identical after the change, and the `--specs` run object's own properties never include `storeReport`/`changeScope`
   286	
   287	### Requirement: verify --change carries the modified-block integrity report
   288	On a trustworthy `--change` run, `verify` SHALL attach the integrity engine's report for every MODIFIED operation (rename-then-modify targets included, keyed by the post-rename name with the pre-rename block as baseline). The old blocks' scenario titles ride the EXISTING single title batch as a fourth tag class that never enters binding accumulation, scenario counts or the change scope — the two-batch matcher contract stands. JSON: `modifiedIntegrity` is ALWAYS present on GREEN/GAPS (one entry per MODIFIED operation, empty-diff entries included; `file` = store-relative suffix; element shapes, value choices and orderings per the engine's contract; missingLines carry full untruncated text) and ABSENT on every ERROR class and on every `--specs` run (byte goldens continue to prove the latter). Human: a `— MODIFIED INTEGRITY —` section prints an entry when any of dropped/missingLines/ambiguous/titleChanged is non-empty (`!` prefix on dropped/missingLines/ambiguous lines; a plain one-line note per titleChanged; counts only for retained/added), stays entirely silent when every entry's four classes are empty, and truncates lines the state-A way (`slice(0,119)+'…'`). The report never changes the verdict or the exit code.
   289	
   290	#### Scenario: SR-65 the report rides the run without touching the verdict
   291	- WHEN a `--change` run's MODIFIED replacement drops KV-02 and loses one AND line, while every scoped scenario is bound green (TAP covering the replacement's scenarios, exit 0)
   292	- THEN the run is still GREEN exit 0; `modifiedIntegrity` carries dropped=[KV-02] and the missing line with full text; the human section prints them with `!`; and removing one scoped test flips the same run to GAPS exit 1 with the identical report attached
   293	
   294	#### Scenario: SR-66 presence follows the outcome class
   295	- WHEN `--change --json` runs end GREEN, GAPS, pre-test ERROR and post-TAP ERROR, and a `--specs --json` run executes
   296	- THEN GREEN/GAPS carry `modifiedIntegrity` (an ADDED-only delta yields `[]`; an idempotent MODIFIED — the trim-equal path AND the stamped-rerun repaired path alike — yields its empty-diff entry); both ERROR classes and the `--specs` run omit the field entirely (absent, never null)
   297	
   298	#### Scenario: SR-67 the old-block titles join the one batch and bind nothing
   299	- WHEN a config-origin `--change` run with a MODIFIED delta executes with a counting child seam
   300	- THEN the matcher child still runs EXACTLY twice, the first payload contains the OLD block's titles alongside the projection and sibling titles, and the old titles appear in no binding class, no scenario count, no change scope and no store report
   301	
   302	#### Scenario: SR-68 the frozen live-specimen fixture reports exactly as hand-derived
   303	- WHEN the engine runs over the frozen copy of the verify-change-scope change's real MODIFIED block (the nine-scenario `projected verify` requirement) against its frozen old store block
   304	- THEN the report deep-equals the hand-derived expectation: retained = ALL NINE scenarios (the two rewrites changed only THEN lines, never titles — hand derivation corrected the earlier assumption), titleChanged=[], dropped=[], added=[], ambiguous=[], and missingLines = exactly the two replaced old THEN lines (scenario SR-16's and scenario SR-18's), full text
   305	
   306	### Requirement: a scoped verdict judges a named scenario set, nothing else
   307	`lib/spec-runner.js` SHALL expose `scopedEvaluate(byId, results, scope)`: given the store projection, one TAP snapshot and an explicit set of scenario IDs, it classifies ONLY the scope — each scoped ID lands in bound-green, bound-red or unbound by the same rules the whole-store verdict uses (a scenario whose only results are skips is UNBOUND), and the verdict is clean exactly when the scope carries no red and no unbound member. Store-wide classes are NOT scope classes: results outside the scope are never orphans of a scoped run, and store-wide unidentified scenarios never dirty it — the whole-store verdict keeps its own meaning, unchanged. A scope naming an ID absent from the projection is a caller error surfaced as `missing`, never a silent pass. An empty scope is clean by construction and says so.
   308	
   309	#### Scenario: SR-69 the scope is judged and the rest of the store is not
   310	- WHEN the store carries five scenarios of which two are red and one unbound, and the scope names one green scenario
   311	- THEN the scoped verdict is clean with that one scenario bound-green — the store's red and unbound members are outside the scope and outside the verdict
   312	
   313	#### Scenario: SR-70 red and unbound inside the scope dirty the scoped verdict
   314	- WHEN the scope names a scenario whose TAP result fails, and separately one with no result at all, and separately one whose only results are skips
   315	- THEN each scoped verdict is unclean, classifying the member as bound-red, unbound and unbound respectively
   316	
   317	#### Scenario: SR-71 out-of-scope results are not orphans and an empty scope is clean
   318	- WHEN the TAP snapshot carries results for IDs the scope does not name (some absent from the store entirely), and separately the scope is empty
   319	- THEN the first verdict reports no orphans and stays clean; the second is clean by construction with every class empty
   320	
   321	#### Scenario: SR-72 a scope member absent from the projection is a caller error
   322	- WHEN the scope names an ID that the store projection does not carry
   323	- THEN the scoped verdict reports it under `missing` and is unclean — an unknown target is never silently satisfied
   324	
   325	### Requirement: the change projection is one shared implementation, reachable without running tests
   326	The delta-to-store projection SHALL be built by exactly ONE implementation, reachable by every consumer through a single replaceable reference. `spec-runner` SHALL export that reference resolver alongside the builder, and `verify` SHALL itself go through it — so that a consumer which needs the projection WITHOUT executing a test command (gate's skipped-C1 path) provably shares `verify`'s code, its inputs, and its failure classes. Two test-only injection seams SHALL exist: one over the projection builder and one over the test-command runner. Each SHALL default to the real function, and installing an override and clearing it SHALL leave a configured run's complete public results structurally identical — the claim is that observable three-state equality, NOT byte-identity against any earlier implementation, which no test inside this repo can observe.
   327	
   328	#### Scenario: SR-73 both consumers pass through the same replaceable builder
   329	- WHEN a test wraps the projection builder through its seam and then runs BOTH `verify --change` (with a test command) AND the projection-only path (without one)
   330	- THEN both calls pass through the SAME wrapper — the reference is read at call time, never captured at module load — and the wrapper observes identical `{projection: {modules, conflicts, unstampedMutations, notes}, errors}` for the same change, across clean, unstamped-mutation, malformed-delta, diverged-CAS-base, merge-conflict, and no-delta-files inputs alike; and no consumer reassembles a projection from the underlying delta-discovery primitives instead of going through the shared reference — statically checkable in the consumer's own source
   331	
   332	#### Scenario: SR-74 the projection-only path spawns no test process
   333	- WHEN the projection is built through the projection-only path
   334	- THEN the test-command runner seam records ZERO calls — the projection is produced before, and independently of, any test execution; the id-matcher child seam is a different seam and proves nothing about this
   335	
   336	#### Scenario: SR-75 the seams are inert by default
   337	- WHEN no seam override is installed
   338	- THEN the exported projection resolver returns the real builder itself (directly assertable), the private test-command resolver defaults to the real runner (statically assertable in `spec-runner`'s own source, since it is not exported), and the COMPLETE public results of a configured `verify --change` and a configured `gate` are identical in three states observed in ONE process: before any override exists, after a projection override is installed and cleared, and after a test-runner override is installed and cleared — and while a runner override IS installed the configured result must differ, so the after-clear comparison can never be vacuous. Identity against the PRE-seam implementation is deliberately NOT claimed here: no test inside this repo can observe it, and the pre-existing suite passing unchanged is regression EVIDENCE, not a byte-identity proof.
     1	### Requirement: doctor diagnoses the project-apriori seam
     2	`apriori doctor` SHALL run eight read-only checks (D1 Node floor (≥22) · D2 init scaffold · D3 runbook freshness · D4 tool pointers · D5 TAP probe · D6 store health · D7 changes overview · D8 legacy 3.x layout roots), each reporting ok/finding/not-applicable with a fix hint on findings, and SHALL encode the aggregate in its exit code: 0 = zero findings (`DOCTOR: HEALTHY`), 1 = ≥1 finding (`DOCTOR: <n> finding(s)`), 2 = unusable (Node below floor, `apriori/` missing entirely, positional-arg usage error) — printing whatever checks already ran. Doctor never repairs and never writes; its ONLY side effect is D5 executing the project's test command once, removed by `--no-run`.
     3	
     4	#### Scenario: DR-01 a healthy initialized project reports HEALTHY
     5	- WHEN every applicable check passes on a freshly initialized project with a valid TAP test command
     6	- THEN each check reports ok or not-applicable, the final line is `DOCTOR: HEALTHY`, and the exit code is 0
     7	
     8	#### Scenario: DR-02 an uninitialized project is unusable, with guidance
     9	- WHEN `apriori/` does not exist under the target root
    10	- THEN doctor exits 2, the message names `apriori init`, and `--json` reports `result: "UNUSABLE"` with the already-run checks (D1) listed
    11	
    12	#### Scenario: DR-03 init-scaffold gaps are findings with their fixer
    13	- WHEN `apriori/runbook.md`, `apriori/specs/`, `apriori/.gitignore` (with its `tmp/` line), or the `apriori/tmp/` dir is missing
    14	- THEN D2 emits ONE check entry PER GAP (each with id `D2`, status finding, naming the file and its fixer `apriori init` / `apriori update`) — the aggregate `findings` count equals the number of finding-status entries; a missing `apriori/process-config.md` is not-applicable (optional, defaults apply), never a finding
    15	
    16	#### Scenario: DR-04 runbook freshness points at update, and never contradicts D2
    17	- WHEN the runbook copy differs by any byte from the packaged RUNBOOK.md
    18	- THEN D3 is a finding naming `apriori update`; WHEN the copy is absent THEN D3 is not-applicable ("see D2") — absence is D2's finding alone
    19	
    20	#### Scenario: DR-05 detected tools must keep their pointers
    21	- WHEN a tool that `detectTools` finds has a missing rules file, a rules file without the `apriori/runbook.md` pointer, or (command-level tools) a missing command file
    22	- THEN D4 reports a finding per tool naming the file and `apriori init`; a project with no known tool markers reports not-applicable
    23	
    24	#### Scenario: DR-06 the TAP probe classifies every plumbing edge
    25	- WHEN the test command (from `--test-cmd` or the config row) runs
    26	- THEN D5 classifies: spawn error, signal kill, `Bail out!`, empty output, zero parsed TAP without a version/plan line (naming `--test-reporter=tap`), a TAP version or non-`1..0` plan with zero result lines ("truncated or malformed"), and a non-zero exit unexplained by parsed TAP failures — each a finding; parsed TAP whose failures explain any non-zero exit is ok (counts as info — test failures are NOT doctor findings), and an exactly-`1..0` plan with exit status 0 is ok ("empty suite") — a `1..0` plan with a NON-ZERO exit is the unexplained-exit finding, classified first
    27	
    28	#### Scenario: DR-07 an explicit skip is not-applicable, a missing test command is a finding
    29	- WHEN `--no-run` is given, or the effective id-pattern config is uncompilable
    30	- THEN D5 reports not-applicable with the reason ("probe skipped (--no-run)" / "probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)") — an explicit human skip is never a finding; WHEN instead NO test command is configured anywhere and no `--no-run` was given THEN D5 is a FINDING whose detail names the CONSEQUENCE (`apriori gate` cannot run C1, the binding check, at all) and whose fix names the `test-cmd` row in `apriori/process-config.md`, so the aggregate is `DOCTOR: <n> finding(s)` with exit 1 rather than `HEALTHY`; a CONFLICTING `test-cmd` row stays the pre-existing config finding, whose detail is distinguishable from the missing-command one — while a whole `process-config.md` that cannot be read is classified upstream as an id-pattern problem and lands in the first branch (`n/a`), not here; the branch order is: invalid id-pattern → config problem → `--no-run` → missing command → run the probe
    31	
    32	#### Scenario: DR-08 store health flags unbindable and ambiguous scenarios
    33	- WHEN `apriori/specs/` contains scenarios without a leading default-pattern ID, or the same ID in more than one scenario
    34	- THEN D6 reports findings listing them (detail names the default ID pattern); an empty store or zero scenario files is not-applicable ("normal for a new project"); a missing specs dir is not-applicable here ("see D2")
    35	
    36	#### Scenario: DR-09 changes overview validates flow-states and surfaces pending gates
    37	- WHEN an active change dir has no readable flow-state.md, or `parseFlowState` yields an empty `change`, or its `change` mismatches the dir name
    38	- THEN D7 reports a finding naming the dir and the failed clause; healthy active changes are info lines; archived candidates (basenames matching `<YYYY-MM-DDThhmm>-<name>`, directories only) are read ONLY when they satisfy realpath containment under `apriori/changes/archive/` — an escaping symlinked entry is skipped with an info note, never read; contained not-DONE/ABANDONED archived changes are listed as info ("gate ④ possibly pending") — never a finding
    39	
    40	#### Scenario: DR-10 output is machine-consumable in every class
    41	- WHEN `apriori doctor --json` runs — HEALTHY, FINDINGS, or UNUSABLE (incl. the positional-arg usage error)
    42	- THEN stdout parses as JSON shaped `{ result, findings, checks: [{id, status: "ok"|"finding"|"n/a", detail, fix?}], errors }`
    43	
    44	#### Scenario: DR-11 doctor is read-only and rejects stray arguments
    45	- WHEN doctor runs with `--no-run` against any project tree
    46	- THEN no file is created, modified, or deleted; WHEN any positional argument is given THEN doctor exits 2 with usage (pure JSON under `--json`)
    47	
    48	#### Scenario: DR-12 the Node floor is enforced testably
    49	- WHEN the detected Node major version (injectable for tests) is below 22
    50	- THEN D1 is a finding entry and doctor exits 2 (`result: "UNUSABLE"`), with D1 listed in the checks; at or above 22 → ok
    51	
    52	### Requirement: doctor detects legacy 3.x layout roots
    53	Doctor SHALL check (D8) for the five pre-4.0 scattered roots — top-level `requirement/`, top-level `spike/`, `apriori/review/`, `apriori/design/`, `apriori/explore/` — using existence-level probes only (lstat/exists, never following or reading through); any hit is a finding listing every root found, with a fix pointing at the MIGRATING.md 4.0 section. A clean 4.0 project reports D8 ok; the check performs zero writes.
    54	
    55	#### Scenario: DR-13 mixed 3.x layouts are named, clean ones pass
    56	- WHEN a project carries any of the five legacy roots (a directory or a symlink at that path both count), and another project carries none
    57	- THEN doctor reports a D8 finding naming exactly the roots present with a fix pointing at the migration guidance, and the clean project's D8 is ok — with no file content read and nothing written
    58	
    59	### Requirement: the probe speaks the same TAP as verify
    60	`classifyProbe` SHALL consume the shared version-aware lexer and judge by the D5 matrix: probe TAP containing failures (any shape, unattributed included) is D5 **ok** with a detail noting the failures are verify's business and the TAP channel itself is healthy; an unsupported TAP version is D5 **finding** naming the version and the supported matrix; a stdout-empty probe whose stderr carries TAP-shaped output is D5 **finding** with a `2>&1` fix; every other classification and doctor's exit taxonomy stay unchanged.
    61	
    62	#### Scenario: DR-14 the D5 matrix follows the lexer
    63	- WHEN probes emit: a failing `not ok` with exit 0; a `TAP version 99` stream; TAP on stderr with empty stdout; and a healthy TAP-13 stream
    64	- THEN D5 reports ok (failures noted as verify's business), finding (version named), finding (`2>&1` fix), and ok respectively — with doctor's exit taxonomy unchanged
    65	
    66	### Requirement: doctor reads config through the structured reader
    67	Doctor's config consumption (the D5 probe's test-cmd fallback) SHALL use the shared structured reader; a `test-cmd` CONFLICT is a D5 finding naming the config problem rather than a probe run over an arbitrary row.
    68	
    69	#### Scenario: DR-15 doctor refuses to probe on conflicted config
    70	- WHEN doctor runs (no explicit test command) in a project whose process-config carries two live conflicting `test-cmd` rows
    71	- THEN D5 is a finding naming the config conflict and no probe command executes
    72	
    73	### Requirement: D6 shows its pattern source and diagnoses a bad config row
    74	`doctor`'s D6 SHALL scan the store with the effective id-pattern (config `id-pattern` row > `DEFAULT_ID`; doctor gains no flag) and its detail SHALL name the source — `config` when the row supplied the pattern, `default` otherwise. The id-pattern is resolved AND its D6 store scan is executed BEFORE the D5 probe runs (execution order; the report keeps its display order): an uncompilable config row — or a config-origin scan whose terminable child fails (timeout/kill/crash/malformed output) — makes D6 a `finding` that names `process-config` (fix: repair the row) WITHOUT throwing, AND D5 becomes `n/a` with the deterministic detail `probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)` — the test command is never spawned under a broken id-pattern (the validate-before-any-test-command rule covers doctor too). Absent any other unusable condition the overall result stays `FINDINGS`, exit 1 — doctor diagnoses a repairable config, it does not refuse to run. When the pattern is valid, D5's probe parsing keeps `DEFAULT_ID` (its parsed-count classification is pattern-insensitive; unchanged by this change).
    75	
    76	#### Scenario: DR-16 D6 names its pattern source
    77	- WHEN doctor runs with a valid config `id-pattern` row covering the store's IDs, and separately without any row
    78	- THEN the first D6 is `ok` with a detail naming the config source, and the second names the default source (finding or ok per the store's IDs) — both source texts are asserted
    79	
    80	#### Scenario: DR-17 an invalid config id-pattern is a D6 finding and skips the probe
    81	- WHEN the config `id-pattern` row does not compile and doctor runs on an otherwise healthy project whose test command is a sentinel (it would leave a marker file if spawned)
    82	- THEN D6 is a `finding` naming `process-config` with a repair fix, no scenario scan happens, no exception escapes, D5 is `n/a` with detail `probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)`, the sentinel marker does NOT exist (the command never ran), and the overall result is `FINDINGS` (exit 1)
    83	
    84	#### Scenario: DR-18 a terminated config-pattern scan is a D6 finding and skips the probe
    85	- WHEN the config pattern is catastrophic against the store's titles and doctor runs with a sentinel test command
    86	- THEN the D6 child scan is killed within its budget, D6 is a `finding` naming `process-config`, D5 is `n/a` (`probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)`), the sentinel marker does NOT exist, and the overall result is `FINDINGS` (exit 1)
     1	### Requirement: archive-merge applies delta specs to the living store
     2	`apriori archive` SHALL merge a change's delta requirements into the living spec store by stable Requirement ID, consuming the same `## ADDED/MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED Requirements` delta format the OpenSpec adapter used, so it is the adapter-free native implementation of §4's archive action.
     3	
     4	#### Scenario: AM-01 ADDED appends a new requirement
     5	- WHEN the delta has an ADDED requirement whose ID is absent from the store
     6	- THEN it is appended and the ID is listed as merged
     7	
     8	#### Scenario: AM-02 MODIFIED replaces the existing block
     9	- WHEN the delta has a MODIFIED requirement whose ID exists in the store
    10	- THEN the whole store block is replaced and the ID is listed as modified
    11	
    12	#### Scenario: AM-03 REMOVED marks the block deprecated, not deleted
    13	- WHEN the delta has a REMOVED requirement present in the store
    14	- THEN the store block is kept and marked `deprecated (superseded by <change>)`, listed as deprecated
    15	
    16	#### Scenario: AM-04 same-ID conflict stops without writing
    17	- WHEN an ADDED ID already exists, or a MODIFIED/REMOVED target is missing
    18	- THEN the merge reports the conflict, writes nothing, and exits 1 (a human opens a ledger issue)
    19	
    20	#### Scenario: AM-05 the action lists every merged/modified/deprecated ID
    21	- WHEN a merge succeeds
    22	- THEN every affected Requirement ID is printed by category (§4 requires the action list them)
    23	
    24	#### Scenario: AM-06 archive moves the in-flight change under a dated archive dir
    25	- WHEN archive completes on change `<name>`
    26	- THEN the in-flight `apriori/changes/<name>/` (bare name, no prefix) is moved to `apriori/changes/archive/<YYYY-MM-DDThhmm>-<name>/` — a colon-free date-time stamped by the CLI's own clock (never an agent guess); e.g. `archive/2026-07-06T0657-add-playback/`
    27	
    28	#### Scenario: AM-07 RENAMED renames a requirement in place, preserving content
    29	- WHEN the delta has a `## RENAMED Requirements` entry `- Old Name -> New Name` and `Old Name` exists while `New Name` does not
    30	- THEN the store block keeps its content but its heading ID becomes `New Name`, listed as renamed; a missing source (with no existing target) or a colliding target is a conflict (stop, exit 1, nothing written) — the source-gone-AND-target-present rerun case is AM-10's no-op, not a conflict
    31	
    32	#### Scenario: AM-08 a content-bearing delta that parses to zero operations is a hard error
    33	- WHEN `apriori archive` reads a delta file that has non-whitespace content but matches no recognized section (e.g. wrong heading level or keyword, so 0 delta operations parse)
    34	- THEN it writes nothing, prints an error naming the expected `## ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED|RENAMED Requirements` + `### Requirement:` format, and exits non-zero — never reporting MERGED for a delta the parser did not understand
    35	
    36	#### Scenario: AM-09 the first archive in a repo creates the store file
    37	- WHEN `apriori archive --write` targets a store file that does not exist yet
    38	- THEN it notes the store will be created, starts from an empty store, and writes the merged result — no ENOENT
    39	
    40	#### Scenario: AM-10 re-running an already-merged delta is an idempotent no-op
    41	- WHEN an ADDED requirement in the delta already exists in the store with byte-identical (trimmed) content, or a RENAMED entry's source is gone while its target is present, or a REMOVED entry's target is gone while its deprecated form marked `superseded by <THIS change>` is present — each the signature of a rerun after a partial archive
    42	- THEN each is reported as already merged / already renamed / already deprecated (no-op), not a conflict, and the run can proceed to move the change dir; but an ADDED whose name was created by THIS run's own RENAMED is a same-delta collision and still conflicts even with identical content, and a REMOVED target deprecated by a DIFFERENT change remains a conflict
    43	
    44	#### Scenario: AM-11 change names are validated and the dir move cannot escape
    45	- WHEN `--change` is not bare kebab-case (e.g. `../victim`), or `--changes-dir` is given but the change dir does not exist
    46	- THEN archive exits 2 before writing anything; the move helper independently rejects any source that resolves outside the changes dir
    47	
    48	#### Scenario: AM-12 the store commit and the dir move are one transaction (single-file form)
    49	- WHEN the single-file form `apriori archive --store <f> --delta <f> --change <name> --write --changes-dir <dir>` runs and the change-dir move fails
    50	- THEN the store on disk stays byte-for-byte untouched (staged to a temp file, committed by rename only after the move succeeds) and no temp residue remains — the high-level `--change` form's move-failure rule is AM-18's (stores already committed stay committed)
    51	
    52	### Requirement: high-level archive merges a whole change transactionally
    53	`apriori archive --change <name>` SHALL discover every `.md` delta file under `<changes-dir>/<name>/specs/` (`--changes-dir` defaults to `apriori/changes` and sets both the discovery root and the move root), map each to its store target `apriori/specs/<suffix>` by path suffix, dry-run the whole set by default, and on `--write` commit in four phases — preflight (validate, parse, CAS-check, merge in memory; any failure anywhere → nothing written), stage (write every `<store>.tmp-archive`), commit (rename each temp in sorted path order), move (only when `--changes-dir` was explicitly passed, only after all stores committed). The guarantee is failure-atomicity up to the commit point; crash durability is not claimed.
    54	
    55	#### Scenario: AM-13 dry-run reports the whole change and writes nothing
    56	- WHEN `apriori archive --change <name>` runs without `--write` on a change spanning several modules
    57	- THEN it prints per-module merged/modified/deprecated/renamed/no-op requirement names and a result line, and no file on disk changes
    58	
    59	#### Scenario: AM-14 any preflight failure means nothing is written
    60	- WHEN any module's merge reports a conflict, any delta file fails a hygiene guard, or any CAS stamp mismatches during `--write`
    61	- THEN every failure across all modules is reported, no store file changes, and the exit code is 1
    62	
    63	#### Scenario: AM-15 a mid-commit failure is reported exactly
    64	- WHEN a rename in the commit phase fails after earlier renames succeeded
    65	- THEN archive reports exactly which modules committed, which did not, and which temp files remain for manual completion, and exits 1 — already-committed renames are not rolled back
    66	
    67	#### Scenario: AM-16 a pre-existing temp file blocks the run
    68	- WHEN any target `<store>.tmp-archive` already exists at preflight
    69	- THEN archive exits 1 naming that file, writes nothing, and leaves the pre-existing file untouched (it may be another run in flight or a manual-recovery artifact)
    70	
    71	#### Scenario: AM-17 zero discovered delta files fail closed
    72	- WHEN `<changes-dir>/<name>/specs/` exists but contains no `.md` delta files, or the change dir itself is missing
    73	- THEN archive exits 2 naming the searched path
    74	
    75	#### Scenario: AM-18 the change-dir move waits for every store commit
    76	- WHEN `--write --changes-dir <dir>` succeeds
    77	- THEN the change dir moves to `<dir>/archive/<stamp>-<name>/` only after ALL stores committed; without an explicit `--changes-dir` no move happens; a move failure leaves the committed stores in place and exits 1
    78	
    79	#### Scenario: AM-19 high-level and single-file forms are mutually exclusive
    80	- WHEN `--change` is combined with `--store` or `--delta`
    81	- THEN archive exits 2 with usage; the single-file form by itself keeps its 3.0.1 behavior unchanged
    82	
    83	#### Scenario: AM-20 per-file delta hygiene guards the whole set
    84	- WHEN any discovered delta file is empty/whitespace-only, parses to zero operations despite content, or carries duplicate requirement names (within a section or across sections)
    85	- THEN the run refuses naming the file (and requirement, for duplicates), nothing is written, and no partial projection or merge survives
    86	
    87	#### Scenario: AM-21 duplicate requirement names in the store are corruption
    88	- WHEN the store text handed to any merge contains the same requirement name twice
    89	- THEN the merge reports it as a conflict instead of silently keeping the last block
    90	
    91	#### Scenario: AM-22 realpath containment governs every participating path
    92	- WHEN the change dir, a delta file, a mapped store target, or the move destination (`<changes-dir>/archive/…` — e.g. an `archive/` that is a symlink pointing outside) — each followed through symlinks — resolves outside its root (changes dir / `apriori/specs/`), or a not-yet-existing path's nearest existing ancestor resolves outside its root
    93	- THEN the command exits 2 naming the offending path, and nothing is read as spec input, written, or moved
    94	
    95	### Requirement: CAS base stamps detect store divergence
    96	A delta file MAY carry exactly one base stamp `<!-- apriori-base: sha256:<64 lowercase hex> -->` or `<!-- apriori-base: new -->` before its first `## <OP> Requirements` section, declaring the fingerprint of the store file it was authored against. The fingerprint SHALL be SHA-256 over the store file's content with line endings normalized (`\r\n` and lone `\r` → `\n`). `apriori verify --change`, `apriori archive --change`, and single-file `apriori archive` SHALL check present stamps; an absent stamp means no check. `apriori stamp <store-file>` SHALL print the stamp line matching the file's current content.
    97	
    98	#### Scenario: AM-23 malformed or duplicated stamps are hygiene errors
    99	- WHEN a delta file carries more than one stamp line, a stamp after the first section heading, or a malformed digest
   100	- THEN the file is rejected by the hygiene guard (nothing projected or written) naming the file
   101	
   102	#### Scenario: AM-24 a diverged stamp stops archive before any write, on both surfaces
   103	- WHEN a stamped delta's store file fingerprint no longer matches (including a `new` stamp whose store now exists) — whether archive is invoked as `--change <name>` or as single-file `--store <f> --delta <f>`
   104	- THEN each form reports the store path with expected vs actual fingerprint, writes nothing, and exits 1 — the §4.11 serialize rule made mechanical; both surfaces are exercised, not just one
   105	
   106	#### Scenario: AM-25 stamp-free ADDED-only deltas keep the pre-3.1 behavior
   107	- WHEN a delta carries no base stamp — in the high-level `--change` form and in the single-file form alike
   108	- THEN no CAS divergence check runs for that file; an ADDED-only delta merges exactly as pre-3.1, while a stamp-free MUTATION delta falls to the deny-by-default rule (its own requirement) — "no stamp, no check" never means "no stamp, no rules"
   109	
   110	#### Scenario: AM-26 the new sentinel matches only an absent store
   111	- WHEN a delta is stamped `<!-- apriori-base: new -->`
   112	- THEN the check passes while the mapped store file does not exist and fails as a mismatch once it does
   113	
   114	#### Scenario: AM-27 apriori stamp prints the current stamp line
   115	- WHEN `apriori stamp <store-file>` runs on an existing readable file, an absent path, a directory, or with a wrong argument count
   116	- THEN it prints the exact matching stamp line (absent path → the `new` form) and exits 0; a directory or unreadable file → error naming the path, exit 2; zero or multiple arguments → usage, exit 2
   117	
   118	### Requirement: the delta parser consumes its whole input
   119	`parseDeltaStrict` SHALL be a sequential, fully-consuming parser: every line (outside code fences, which are opaque) belongs to exactly one legal construct — file preamble (stamp/blank/free text without structure markers), a legal `## ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED|RENAMED Requirements` heading, section-preamble free text, a `### Requirement:` block (body runs to the next heading or EOF), or a RENAMED `- Old -> New` line — and anything else is a `problems[]` entry carrying its 1-based line number. The exhaustive problem set: unrecognized h2 headings (one problem per heading; its lines are never absorbed into any bucket), requirement/scenario markers before any section, a requirement block inside RENAMED, a scenario marker outside any requirement, duplicate requirement names, and the stamp problems (which also gain line numbers). Both `verify --change` (exit 2) and `archive` (exit 1) inherit the failure.
   120	
   121	#### Scenario: AM-28 a misspelled section heading is reported, never absorbed
   122	- WHEN a delta contains `## ADDDED Requirements` (or any other unrecognized h2) followed by requirement blocks
   123	- THEN parsing reports the heading text with its line number, those requirements appear in NO bucket, and archive/verify fail closed
   124	
   125	#### Scenario: AM-29 structure outside its legal home is reported with line numbers
   126	- WHEN a `### Requirement:` appears before any section or inside a RENAMED section, or a `#### Scenario:` appears in a section preamble outside any requirement
   127	- THEN each is a problem carrying its 1-based line number; multiple distinct problems are all reported
   128	
   129	#### Scenario: AM-30 free text and fences stay legal; the corpus stays clean
   130	- WHEN a delta carries titles/notes in the file preamble, section preambles, or requirement bodies, or fenced content containing heading-like lines
   131	- THEN no problem is reported — and every archived delta in this repo's `apriori/changes/archive/*/specs/` parses with zero problems (the regression corpus)
   132	
   133	#### Scenario: AM-31 stamp problems carry line numbers
   134	- WHEN a delta carries a malformed, duplicated, or misplaced apriori-base stamp
   135	- THEN each stamp problem names its 1-based line number
   136	
   137	### Requirement: unstamped mutation deltas are denied by default and reruns of applied stamps repair
   138	A delta carrying MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED operations without a CAS stamp SHALL be **denied by default on every archive surface** — the high-level `archive --change` form AND the single-file `--store --delta` form: preflight-grade error (exit 1) naming the file and the cure (`apriori stamp <store-file>`), with **nothing written, staged, or moved**. A visible waiver downgrades to the former warn-and-merge behavior: the archive CLI's `--no-cas` flag or a `| cas | optional |` row in `apriori/process-config.md` (the flag wins), and the output names which waiver source applied. ADDED-only deltas stay exempt (the exemption is clobber-focused: they conflict or no-op, never silently overwrite). `verify --change` (the read-only projection) keeps warning without judging; `buildProjection` still returns `unstampedMutations: string[]`. `merge()`'s MODIFIED op SHALL report `unchanged` when the delta block trim-equals the current store block. Archive preflight SHALL classify each stamped delta per file: stamp matches → merge as today; stamp mismatches with EVERY op `unchanged` → a note (`rerun accepted`) and the run proceeds, move included; stamp mismatches with ANY real pending op → error, the whole preflight fails with nothing written or moved — divergence with pending work is never repaired, and notes stay distinct from errors in the diagnostics.
   139	
   140	#### Scenario: AM-32 unstamped mutation deltas are denied on both archive forms
   141	- WHEN an unstamped delta carrying a MODIFIED op is archived via --change or via --store/--delta with no waiver
   142	- THEN each form errors (exit 1) naming the file and the stamp cure, writes/stages/moves nothing, and an unstamped ADDED-only delta merges without complaint
   143	
   144	#### Scenario: AM-40 the waiver is visible and downgrades to warn-and-merge
   145	- WHEN the same unstamped MODIFIED delta is archived with `--no-cas`, or with a `| cas | optional |` process-config row
   146	- THEN the merge happens with the former warning, the output names the waiver source that applied, and with both present the flag wins
   147	
   148	#### Scenario: AM-41 the projection surface stays informative
   149	- WHEN `verify --change` projects a change containing an unstamped mutation delta
   150	- THEN it warns exactly as before and never fails the run for the missing stamp — denial is an archive-surface rule
   151	
   152	#### Scenario: AM-33 an already-applied stamped delta reruns to completion
   153	- WHEN a stamped change whose commit already rewrote the stores reruns `archive --write --changes-dir` (an already-applied MODIFIED-only delta included)
   154	- THEN every mismatched file reports the rerun-accepted note, stores stay byte-identical, the change dir moves, and the exit is 0
   155	
   156	#### Scenario: AM-34 divergence with pending work never repairs
   157	- WHEN a stamped delta's base mismatches and at least one operation is a real pending change — including the mixed case where another file is already applied
   158	- THEN preflight fails with the mismatch error, nothing is written or moved, and the diagnostics keep already-applied notes apart from divergence errors — while the pure resumed-partial-commit mix (applied-mismatched file + matching file with real ops) completes with the note
   159	
   160	#### Scenario: AM-35 MODIFIED speaks the idempotence vocabulary
   161	- WHEN a MODIFIED operation's delta block trim-equals the current store block
   162	- THEN merge reports it `unchanged` rather than `modified`, and first applications behave exactly as before
   163	
   164	### Requirement: archive stages the requirement history into the change before the move  _deprecated (superseded by change-bundle)_
   165	With `--write` and an explicit `--changes-dir`, `archiveChange` SHALL, AFTER the stores commit and BEFORE the change-dir move, rename every file in `<cwd>/requirement/` whose basename exactly matches one of three anchored patterns for the change — `^<change>-req-v[0-9]+\.md$`, `^<change>-req-final\.md$`, `^<change>-intent-card\.md$` (change name regex-escaped) — into `changes/<name>/requirement/` (created on demand, basenames preserved), so the existing atomic move carries the requirement history; no post-move writes exist. Fail-closed: the source dir must realpath-resolve inside cwd and the destination must pass containment under the changes dir before any read or rename; a MATCHING candidate that is a symlink (lstat) fails the run before the move, naming the symlink and the cure — symlinks are never followed, non-matching entries are ignored. Any staging failure aborts before the move with exit 1 and the existing rerunnable taxonomy (`stores committed but requirement staging failed … — rerun to complete`); a rerun completes staging and the move (stores re-verify as applied, staged files no-op). Zero matches → silent no-op; dry-run and the single-file form never stage. The report carries `staged: <n> requirement file(s) → changes/<name>/requirement/` when n > 0.
   166	
   167	#### Scenario: AM-36 the requirement history travels inside the atomic move
   168	- WHEN a change with `<change>-req-v1.md`, `-req-v2.md`, `-req-final.md`, and `-intent-card.md` in requirement/ archives with --write --changes-dir
   169	- THEN all four end inside the archived dir's requirement/, the live requirement/ no longer has them, the report carries the staged-line, and the exit is 0
   170	
   171	#### Scenario: AM-37 attribution is exact and near-misses never match
   172	- WHEN changes `a` and `a-b` both own requirement files and `a` archives, with `a-req-vdraft.md` and `a-req-v1-notes.md` also present
   173	- THEN only `a`'s three-pattern matches are staged; `a-b-*` and both near-misses stay untouched — and an intent-card-only change stages just the card with exit 0
   174	
   175	#### Scenario: AM-38 staging failures stop before the move and reruns complete
   176	- WHEN a staging rename is made to fail (DI seam), or a MATCHING candidate is a symlink, or the staging destination `changes/<name>/requirement` resolves outside the change tree (a planted symlink), or the source `requirement/` dir itself escapes cwd
   177	- THEN archive exits 1 BEFORE the move (stores committed, change dir still in flight, nothing read or written outside the workspace) naming the offender and the cure, and a rerun after the cure completes staging plus the move to the AM-36 end state
   178	
   179	#### Scenario: AM-39 non-staging paths are unaffected
   180	- WHEN a change has zero matching requirement files, or runs dry-run, or omits --changes-dir, or uses the single-file form
   181	- THEN no staging happens, no staged-line appears, and behavior is byte-identical to before
   182	
   183	### Requirement: the atomic move carries the whole bundle
   184	`archiveChange` SHALL have no staging phase and no post-commit writes of any kind: with `--write` and an explicit `--changes-dir`, the change dir — which by the bundle layout already contains `requirement/`, `review/`, `gap-report.md`, and everything else the change owns — moves to `archive/<stamp>-<name>/` in the existing single atomic rename, carrying it all. The command reads and writes nothing under any legacy root; it is track-agnostic and never deletes `spike/` (executor protocol). Dry-run and the single-file form behave as before.
   185	
   186	#### Scenario: AM-36 the bundle travels whole
   187	- WHEN a bundle change carrying requirement/ (req versions + intent card), review/ (ledger, docs, raws), and gap-report.md archives with --write --changes-dir
   188	- THEN the archived dir contains all of them byte-identically, nothing is left behind in the live changes dir, no staging or copy lines appear in the report, and the exit is 0
   189	
   190	#### Scenario: AM-37 the command touches nothing outside the moved dir
   191	- WHEN the same archive runs
   192	- THEN no file outside `changes/<name>/` and the store files is read for staging or written — there is no requirement-staging code path at all
   193	
   194	#### Scenario: AM-38 move failure keeps the bundle intact and rerunnable
   195	- WHEN the dir move is made to fail (DI seam)
   196	- THEN stores stay committed, the untouched bundle remains in flight with all its contents, the existing move-failure taxonomy reports the rerun cure, and a rerun completes the move with the bundle intact
   197	
   198	#### Scenario: AM-39 non-move paths are unaffected
   199	- WHEN a change runs dry-run, omits --changes-dir, or uses the single-file form
   200	- THEN behavior is byte-identical to before — no move, no bundle handling
   201	
   202	### Requirement: archive consults CAS config through the structured reader
   203	`archive`'s waiver lookup SHALL go through the shared structured config reader: fenced/commented rows grant nothing, a live row decides, and a cas CONFLICT or illegal value at consultation is a preflight error (exit 1, nothing written or moved) naming the config problem — bad config never equals a waiver; `--no-cas` keeps explicit supremacy; stamped or ADDED-only runs never consult the key, so a bad row cannot affect them.
   204	
   205	#### Scenario: AM-42 the reviewer's fenced-waiver bypass is dead
   206	- WHEN an unstamped MODIFIED delta archives under a config whose fenced block says `| cas | optional |` while the live row says `| cas | required |` (the reviewer's reproduced bypass), and again under a cas CONFLICT, and again fully stamped under the same broken configs
   207	- THEN the first two runs are DENIED at preflight (nothing written; the conflict named in the second) while the stamped run proceeds untouched — and `--no-cas` still waives the first two explicitly
   208	
   209	### Requirement: the modified-block integrity engine reports replacement fidelity
   210	A pure shared helper SHALL compare a MODIFIED operation's old store block against its replacement (both captured at the projection's own parse snapshot — `buildProjection` additionally returns the raw old/new block text pairs per MODIFIED operation; no consumer re-reads files) and report structural fidelity, WITHOUT changing any verdict, exit code or write semantics. Structure scan: the requirement heading line never participates; requirement prose runs from the line after the heading to the first VALID scenario heading (state A's actual regex `/^####\s+Scenario:\s+(.*)$/m`, fence-aware exactly like `stripFences` — only lines inside CLOSED triple-backtick pairs are not delimiters; an unclosed opener is ordinary text and later headings still split); fence delimiter lines and fenced non-empty lines are compared as body lines. Pairing runs per key (scenario ID when present, normalized full title otherwise) through one cardinality truth table — o=0,n=1 added; o=1,n=0 dropped; o=1,n=1 paired (same normalized title → retained, different → titleChanged, mutually exclusive, both body-compared); any side >1 → ambiguous (side old/new/both with counts; the key skips every other class and body comparison). Line comparison is ONE algorithm: order-preserving greedy subsequence over normalized non-empty lines (CRLF/CR→LF, trailing whitespace stripped, leading whitespace stripped; duplicates consumed by count); unmatched OLD lines are `missingLines` (requirement prose misses carry scenario=null; only old-content loss is reported). An idempotent replacement takes a FAST PATH — `oldBlock.trim() === newBlock.trim()` short-circuits before any structure scan or id extraction — yielding an entry whose diff arrays are all empty (the stamped-rerun repair path included: a repaired module still contributes its equal-text pair, one entry per MODIFIED operation always). Capture points are module-level testable: `buildProjection().modifiedBlocks` carries raw text pairs — for rename-then-modify the oldBlock deep-equals the COMPLETE pre-rename block (captured before the rename key swap) and the newBlock deep-equals the delta block. The human formatter renders SAFELY: every externally-sourced field (titles, lines, requirement names, file suffixes, ambiguous keys) has C0/DEL control characters replaced with `·` BEFORE the 120-unit truncation (JSON keeps full raw text; JSON.stringify escapes there); an all-empty result renders as NOTHING (no blank line).
   211	
   212	#### Scenario: AM-43 the cardinality truth table classifies every occurrence
   213	- WHEN an old block carries KV-01/KV-02/KV-03 and the replacement carries KV-01 (same title), KV-03 (changed title), KV-04 — and separately each of the eight o/n cardinality rows is exercised (including a duplicated KV-05 on one side and no-ID title keys)
   214	- THEN retained=[KV-01], titleChanged=[KV-03 with old/new titles], dropped=[KV-02], added=[KV-04]; each truth-table row lands in its one class; ambiguous entries carry {key, side, oldCount, newCount} and skip every other class; ambiguous ordering is old-present keys by old first occurrence, then new-only keys by new first occurrence
   215	
   216	#### Scenario: AM-44 the subsequence comparison reports lost lines faithfully
   217	- WHEN a retained scenario's replacement drops one AND clause, reorders two lines, repeats a line fewer times, differs only in indentation/trailing whitespace on another, and the requirement prose loses a sentence
   218	- THEN missingLines lists the dropped AND clause, the reordered-out line and the under-repeated line (by count) with full original text, the whitespace-only difference is NOT reported, and the prose loss carries scenario=null
   219	
   220	#### Scenario: AM-45 structure boundaries follow state A exactly
   221	- WHEN a rename-then-modify replacement preserves prose and scenarios verbatim under the new requirement name, a CLOSED fence contains a scenario-looking heading, an UNCLOSED fence precedes a real scenario heading, and a heading uses multiple spaces after `####`
   222	- THEN the rename case reports zero missingLines (the heading line is never compared); the fenced fake heading does not split a scenario and its lines are body-compared; the heading after the unclosed opener still splits (stripFences strips only closed pairs); the multi-space heading is a valid delimiter
   223	
   224	#### Scenario: AM-46 archive prints the integrity section without changing its semantics
   225	- WHEN `apriori archive --change <name>` (the high-level form only; the single-file form is out of scope) passes preflight with a MODIFIED delta whose replacement drops a scenario — on dry-run and on `--write`
   226	- THEN the same human integrity section appears in the output BEFORE the write-result lines (an output-order guarantee — archive's output stays buffered as in state A; physical I/O timing is not promised), the RESULT line, exit code and written bytes are identical to a run without the report, and ANY preflight failure — malformed, CAS, conflict, denial, a pre-existing temp file, an escaping --changes-dir destination — prints NO integrity section
   227	
   228	#### Scenario: AM-47 the archive id-pattern channel is resolved, terminable and degradable
   229	- WHEN archive runs with a custom config `id-pattern` row recognizing letter-suffixed IDs; with no row (default); with an uncompilable row; and with an injected matcher failure
   230	- THEN the first two produce correct reports through the injected terminable matcher — the pinned seam: `archiveMerge.cli(argv, deps)` and `archiveChange({..., idMatcherFactory})` accept an optional factory `(cwd) => matcher | {error}`; bin builds it lazily from `lib/config`'s resolveIdPattern plus spec-runner's makeIdMatcher; a MISSING factory degrades exactly like a factory error (warning + skip); archive-merge itself never requires spec-runner; the last two print exactly one stderr warning — the WHOLE line `warning: modified-integrity <reason>` passed through sanitizeMsg (control chars replaced with `·`, ≤200 UTF-16 units) — skip the report, and leave every other archive output and exit unchanged

     7	## D1 `lib/config.js` — 一个常量
     8	
     9	```
    10	:16   const DEFAULT_ID = '[A-Z]+-\\d+';
    11	   →  const DEFAULT_ID = '[A-Z]+(?:-[A-Z]+)*-\\d+[a-z]*';
    12	```
    13	
    14	**为什么是严格超集**：对旧式能匹配的任一输入，新式在同一起点吃掉的子串**逐字节相同**——
    15	`(?:-[A-Z]+)*` 与 `[a-z]*` 都能匹配空。已用本仓 store 实测（353/0/0，ID 集合逐个相同）。
    16	
    17	**为什么后缀这一段是必要的**：`leadId` 在正则之后还查边界
    18	（`next` 若是 `[A-Za-z0-9_]` 则作废），所以只放宽段数、不放宽后缀的话，`AC-30f` 仍然绑不上。

    60	
    61	`NOTES_LINE_RE = /^##\s+Notes\s*$/`，**必须在 `SECTION_LINE_RE` 与 `H2_LINE_RE` 之间**判：
    62	
    63	```
    64	:126   if (sec) { … }                          // 四种法定操作段
    65	       ← 在此插入：if (NOTES_LINE_RE.test(line)) { flush(); state='IN_NOTES'; kind=null; continue; }
    66	:127   if (H2_LINE_RE.test(line)) { … }        // 其它 h2 → problem + SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED

    68	
    69	放这个位置就自动满足三件事：
    70	① `## Notes` 不再落进「未识别 h2」分支；

    75

    78	```

    91	  problems.push(`line ${n}: '${line.trim()}' is not a requirement heading — put explanatory
    92	                 content in a '## Notes' section`);

    94	  continue;
    95	}

    97	
    98	**三条不可动的边界**：
    99	
   100	| # | 约束 | 理由 |
   101	|---|---|---|
   102	| 1 | 必须在**戳处理（`:135-144`）之后** | 状态 A 保证「游离的戳绝不被当正文吸收」；放到戳之前会吞掉作废块内的戳，打破「RENAMED 逐字节不变」（r3 的教训） |
   103	| 2 | `!blockDiscard` 是判定的一部分（**P1**） | 已作废的块内不再产出第二条 problem；同时让**既有的** RENAMED 作废块行为一字不变 |
   104	| 3 | 只判 `IN_REQUIREMENT` | `FILE_PREAMBLE` / `IN_SECTION` / `SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED` / `IN_NOTES` 一律保持状态 A |

 succeeded in 103ms:
     1	# proposal — id-pattern-and-delta-notes
     2	
     3	## WHY
     4	
     5	三件事，都是「**默认值与语法把合法输入判成了缺陷**」。
     6	
     7	1. **默认 id-pattern 认不出真实项目的 ID**。`[A-Z]+-\d+` 认不出 `AC-BIS-01`、`LIFE-DWS-01`、`AC-30f`。
     8	   棕地样本实测：151 个场景里 **50 个**绑不上——而那些 ID 完全合法（唯一、稳定、可被测试名携带）。
     9	   后果链：verify 报 GAPS → gate C1 BLOCKED → 每个 change 都要手写一段「这是结构局限不是缺陷」的免责说明。
    10	   4.1.0 把 pattern 变成可配置了，**但默认值没动**，而配置行住在人类持有的 `process-config.md` 里——
    11	   实测那个项目升到 4.1 后该文件仍是模板原样，**从没人加过那一行**。修复躺在二进制里，落地率 0。
    12	2. **doctor D6 的修复提示指错对象**。它说 `add leading IDs`，会把人打发去改 50 个合法 ID，
    13	   而正解是加一行 config（本 change 之后：什么都不用做）。
    14	3. **delta 语法里没有说明性内容的位置**。作者写的小节标题被当成指令而阻断归档；
    15	   更糟的是 **`### 非-Requirement 标题` 落在 requirement 块里会被静默并进 store**——
    16	   MODIFIED 路径上 4.1.0 的完整性报告能照出来，**ADDED 路径上照不出来**。
    17	
    18	## WHAT
    19	
    20	1. 默认式放宽为 `[A-Z]+(?:-[A-Z]+)*-\d+[a-z]*`——旧式的**严格超集**，对旧式能识别的每一个输入
    21	   返回**逐字节相同**的子串（本仓 store 实测：新旧两式的 identified/unidentified/duplicates 与 ID 集合完全相同）。
    22	2. **doctor D5 与项目的场景 ID 词汇表解耦**：它判的是 TAP **管道**，不是身份，却借用了 `DEFAULT_ID`。
    23	   不解耦的话，放宽默认式会让 `ok 1 - AC-30f x # SKIP` 这类行从 untagged 变成 tagged skip，
    24	   使 `parsed` 归零，把健康的 TAP 打成 `truncated or malformed`。
    25	3. **D6 按类分流**：首 token 含数字且含 `-`/`_` = ID 形状 → fix 指向 `id-pattern` 配置行；否则 → 维持原文案。
    26	4. **delta 新增 `## Notes` 不透明段**，并把「requirement 块内的非-Requirement h3」从静默并入改成带行号的 problem。
    27	
    28	## OUT OF SCOPE
    29	
    30	| 不做 | 理由 |
    31	|---|---|
    32	| 改 `resolveIdPattern` 的优先级（flag > config > 默认） | `gate-id-pattern` 刚立的契约，本 change 只换兜底值 |
    33	| 替用户往 `process-config.md` 写 `id-pattern` 行 | 违反 R3；而且本 change 的**要点**正是让大多数项目不需要那一行 |
    34	| 改 `leadId` 的边界规则 | 与 pattern 宽窄正交，改它会动每一个消费者的绑定语义 |
    35	| 让解析器「智能判断」哪些标题是说明 | 复盘明确列为过度方案。一个约定好的段名就够 |
    36	| 修 D5 把 tagged SKIP/TODO 算成 0 这个**既有**怪癖 | 它今天就存在（旧 pattern 认得的 ID 一样中招），与本 change 无因果；修它要改 D5 的分类语义，有自己的验收面。本 change 用**解耦**保证不把它变严重 |
    37	| 讨论模板该不该给 `id-pattern` 设 active Value | 独立的产品决定（要动 `init` 的模板哲学）；本 change 只保证两列 + 注释三处一致、不留旧值 |
    38	| change 2（`archive-preflight`）的任何内容 | 已按 owner 裁定挂起；本 change 只碰 delta **解析器**，与它改的高层 archive 块不是同一个 Requirement |
    39	
    40	## 必须让人类看见的三件事
    41	
    42	1. **默认值变更是外部可见的行为变更**：既有项目里此前 unidentified 的场景会变成已识别；
    43	   若其中两个恰好识别成同一 ID，会**新出现** duplicate 报错。这是期望行为——
    44	   重复 ID 本来就是缺陷，只是此前被 unidentified 掩盖。
    45	2. **`## Notes` 是新的语法面**，一旦发布就要长期兼容；段名与语义在本 change 里定死。
    46	3. **非-Requirement h3 由「静默并入」变「拒绝」是收紧**。本仓语料实测 0 处受影响，
    47	   但外部项目可能有——CHANGELOG 给出迁移建议（改成 `## Notes`，或降级为正文）。
    48	
    49	## 触及范围
    50	
    51	`lib/config.js`（一个常量）· `lib/doctor.js`（D5 分类 regex 解耦 + D6 fix 分流）·
    52	`lib/archive-merge.js`（`IN_NOTES` 状态 + 一条 h3 判定）· `templates/process-config.md`（三处旧值）。
    53	另：3 份 living spec、3 份 truth、RUNBOOK ×2、docs ×2、CHANGELOG。

     4	> `apriori/specs/…`、`lib/`、`test/`、`templates/`
     5	> 规矩：本步不写代码，只对齐事实。行号取自 `brownfield-round2` @ f415824。

     9	## 一、状态 A — 组件级清点
    10	
    11	### A1 `lib/config.js` — 常量与解析
    12	
    13	| 位置 | 事实 |
    14	|---|---|
    15	| `:16` | `const DEFAULT_ID = '[A-Z]+-\\d+';` —— **唯一**的默认值定义处 |
    16	| `:119-132` | `resolveIdPattern(cwd, flagValue)` → flag（**按存在性**，空 flag 是错误）> config `id-pattern` 行 > `DEFAULT_ID`；返回 `{source, origin}` 或 `{error}` |
    17	| `:156` | 导出 `DEFAULT_ID` 与 `resolveIdPattern` |
    18	
    19	### A2 `DEFAULT_ID` 的全部消费者（grep 实证，共 4 个真实消费点）
    20	
    21	| 消费者 | 位置 | 用途 | 本 change |
    22	|---|---|---|---|
    23	| `resolveIdPattern` 兜底 | `lib/config.js:131` | verify / gate C1 / doctor D6 的生效 pattern | **放宽**（B1） |
    24	| `check` CK-04 | `lib/check.js:163` `checkScenarioIds(specText, name, idPattern = DEFAULT_ID)` | 结构一致性 | 随之放宽 |
    25	| `doctor` D5 探针 | `lib/doctor.js:33` `const idRe = new RegExp(DEFAULT_ID)` | **TAP 行解析** | **解耦**（B1b） |
    26	| `spec-runner` 再导出 | `lib/spec-runner.js:18, :829` | 供 doctor 等取用 | 不变 |
    27	
    28	### A3 D5 的计数为什么会被牵连（req-final §1.5 的源码定位）
    29	
    30	```
    31	lib/doctor.js:45   const parsed = [...results.values()].reduce((s, r) => s + r.pass + r.fail, 0)
    32	                                + untagged.length + unattributedFailures.length;
    33	lib/doctor.js:50   if (parsed === 0) { … 'TAP stream truncated or malformed' … }
    34	```
    35	
    36	`parseTap` 把带 `# SKIP`/`# TODO` 的行记成 `skip`，**既不进 pass/fail、也不进 untagged**。
    37	于是「一个被识别了 ID 的 tagged skip」贡献 0。放宽默认式 ⇒ 更多行被识别 ⇒ 更多行落进这一格。
    38	
    39	**解耦点就是 `:33` 这一行**：把它换成本模块自己的冻结常量即可，`:45` 的算式一字不动。
    40	
    41	### A4 `lib/doctor.js` — D6 的 finding 构造
    42	
    43	| 位置 | 事实 |
    44	|---|---|
    45	| `:147` | `d6Entries.push({ id:'D6', status:'finding', detail: \`scenario(s) without a bindable ${boundedSource(idp.source)} ID${src}: …\`, fix: 'add leading IDs' })` |
    46	| — | detail **已经**回显 bounded source 与来源标记 `src`；本 change 要加的是**按类分流**与第二条 fix |
    47	| `:148` | 重复 ID 的 finding 与本 change 无关，须保持不变（AC-IP-13） |
    48	
    49	### A5 `lib/archive-merge.js` — delta 解析器的真实状态机
    50	
    51	| 位置 | 事实 |
    52	|---|---|
    53	| `:81` | `SECTION_LINE_RE = /^##\s+(ADDED\|MODIFIED\|REMOVED\|RENAMED)\s+Requirements\s*$/` —— **四种**，不是三种 |
    54	| `:82` | `H2_LINE_RE = /^##\s/` |
    55	| `:83` | `REQ_LINE_RE = /^###\s+Requirement:\s+(.+?)\s*$/` |
    56	| `:100` | `let state = 'FILE_PREAMBLE';  // \| 'IN_SECTION' \| 'IN_REQUIREMENT' \| 'SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED'` —— **四个 state**；`kind` 是**正交**变量 |
    57	| `:118-125` | 围栏优先：`inFence` 时一切不透明 |
    58	| `:126` | `SECTION_LINE_RE` 命中 → flush、`state='IN_SECTION'`、`kind=…`、`sectionSeen=true` |
    59	| `:127-132` | 其它 h2 → problem + `state='SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED'`、`kind=null` |
    60	| `:133` | `if (state === 'SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED') continue;` —— **戳也被跳过**（注释明写 "stamps included"） |
    61	| `:135-144` | **`STAMP_ATTEMPT_LINE_RE` 在此**——先于 `REQ_LINE_RE`、先于 body 分支，在**每一个非跳过状态**都生效。这正是 r3 指出的、P1 不得覆盖的那一段 |
    62	| `:145-165` | `REQ_LINE_RE`：段前 → problem；`kind==='RENAMED'` → problem + **作废块**（`blockDiscard=true`）；否则开块（重名 → problem + 作废） |
    63	| `:166` | **`if (state === 'IN_REQUIREMENT') { blockLines.push(line); continue; }`** —— 静默并入的那一行；本 change 要在它**之前**插入新的 h3 判定 |
    64	| `:167` | `SCENARIO_LINE_RE` 在块外 → problem |
    65	| `:168-172` | `IN_SECTION` + `RENAMED` → 解析 `- Old -> New`；其余自由文本 |
    66	| `:375` / `:815` | `deltaOpCount(delta) === 0` → 零操作拒绝（Notes-only 必须仍走这条） |
    67	
    68	**插入点因此非常局部**：`:166` 之前加一条「`state==='IN_REQUIREMENT'` 且 `blockDiscard===false`
    69	且行匹配 `/^###\s/` 且不匹配 `REQ_LINE_RE`」的判定；加一个 `IN_NOTES` 状态与其转移。
    70	
    71	### A6 `templates/process-config.md`（r1·REQ-2 的实证）
    72	
    73	```
    74	| id-pattern | [A-Z]+-\d+ | bare JS regex source …；pipe escaping: … | [A-Z]+-\d+ |
    75	                ^^^^^^^^^^ 第 2 列 Value：init 原样写进新项目，resolveIdPattern 优先消费
    76	                                                                      ^^^^^^^^^^ 第 4 列 Default：说明
    77	```
    78	
    79	表下注释另有一处 `built-in default [A-Z]+-\d+`。**三处旧值都要改**。
    80	
    81	### A7 语料与回归面（实测）
    82	
    83	| 面 | 数字 |
    84	|---|---|

     2	
     3	## MODIFIED Requirements
     4	
     5	### Requirement: D6 shows its pattern source and diagnoses a bad config row
     6	`doctor`'s D6 SHALL scan the store with the effective id-pattern (config `id-pattern` row > `DEFAULT_ID`; doctor gains no flag) and its detail SHALL name the source — `config` when the row supplied the pattern, `default` otherwise. The id-pattern is resolved AND its D6 store scan is executed BEFORE the D5 probe runs (execution order; the report keeps its display order): an uncompilable config row — or a config-origin scan whose terminable child fails (timeout/kill/crash/malformed output) — makes D6 a `finding` that names `process-config` (fix: repair the row) WITHOUT throwing, AND D5 becomes `n/a` with the deterministic detail `probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)` — the test command is never spawned under a broken id-pattern (the validate-before-any-test-command rule covers doctor too). Absent any other unusable condition the overall result stays `FINDINGS`, exit 1 — doctor diagnoses a repairable config, it does not refuse to run. D5's probe parsing SHALL NOT consume `DEFAULT_ID` at all: it keeps its own frozen classification regex, because it judges TAP PLUMBING rather than scenario identity, and borrowing the project's ID vocabulary made a widening of that vocabulary able to reclassify a healthy TAP stream — a tagged SKIP/TODO contributes nothing to the parsed count, so newly-recognised skips could turn `ok` into `truncated or malformed`. The earlier claim that the parsed-count classification is pattern-insensitive was FALSE; decoupling makes it true by construction. D6 SHALL also distinguish WHY a scenario is unbindable: a title whose first whitespace-delimited token contains at least one digit AND at least one `-` or `_` is ID-SHAPED, so its fix names the `id-pattern` row in `apriori/process-config.md`; a title without such a token gets the unchanged add-an-ID fix. Each class produces exactly one finding, and the pattern-mismatch detail carries both the bounded pattern source and its origin.
     7	
     8	#### Scenario: DR-16 D6 names its pattern source
     9	- WHEN doctor runs with a valid config `id-pattern` row covering the store's IDs, and separately without any row
    10	- THEN the first D6 is `ok` with a detail naming the config source, and the second names the default source (finding or ok per the store's IDs) — both source texts are asserted
    11	
    12	#### Scenario: DR-17 an invalid config id-pattern is a D6 finding and skips the probe
    13	- WHEN the config `id-pattern` row does not compile and doctor runs on an otherwise healthy project whose test command is a sentinel (it would leave a marker file if spawned)
    14	- THEN D6 is a `finding` naming `process-config` with a repair fix, no scenario scan happens, no exception escapes, D5 is `n/a` with detail `probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)`, the sentinel marker does NOT exist (the command never ran), and the overall result is `FINDINGS` (exit 1)
    15	
    16	#### Scenario: DR-18 a terminated config-pattern scan is a D6 finding and skips the probe
    17	- WHEN the config pattern is catastrophic against the store's titles and doctor runs with a sentinel test command
    18	- THEN the D6 child scan is killed within its budget, D6 is a `finding` naming `process-config`, D5 is `n/a` (`probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)`), the sentinel marker does NOT exist, and the overall result is `FINDINGS` (exit 1)
    19	
    20	#### Scenario: DR-19 D5 does not consume the project ID vocabulary
    21	- WHEN the effective default scenario-ID pattern is widened and a TAP stream carries a tagged skip whose description uses one of the newly-recognised ID shapes (for example `ok 1 - AC-30f pending # SKIP flaky`)
    22	- THEN D5's classification is IDENTICAL to what it was before the widening — it reads its own frozen regex, never `DEFAULT_ID`, so no widening of the project's ID vocabulary can move a healthy TAP stream into `truncated or malformed`
    23	
    24	#### Scenario: DR-20 D6 tells you which repair it means
    25	- WHEN the store holds scenarios whose titles carry an ID-shaped leading token that the effective pattern does not match, and separately scenarios with no such token at all
    26	- THEN two findings appear, each naming only its own scenarios: the first's fix points at the `id-pattern` row in `apriori/process-config.md` and its detail carries the bounded pattern source and its origin, the second's fix is the unchanged add-a-leading-ID text — a well-formed ID that merely misses the pattern must never send a human off to rewrite fifty IDs

     3	## MODIFIED Requirements
     4	
     5	### Requirement: spec-runner binds scenarios to test runs
     6	`apriori verify` SHALL enumerate scenario IDs from the spec store, run the project's own test command, parse its TAP output, and report each scenario's bound/green state. It SHALL exit 0 iff every identified scenario is bound to at least one test and all its tests pass, with no orphans or unidentified scenarios.
     7	
     8	#### Scenario: SR-01 every scenario bound and green
     9	- WHEN every spec scenario ID appears in a passing test name
    10	- THEN each is reported BOUND-GREEN and the process exits 0 with "spec is the test suite"
    11	
    12	#### Scenario: SR-02 a scenario with no test is UNBOUND
    13	- WHEN a spec scenario ID appears in no test name
    14	- THEN it is reported UNBOUND and the process exits 1
    15	
    16	#### Scenario: SR-03 a failing test makes its scenario BOUND-RED
    17	- WHEN a scenario's test emits `not ok`
    18	- THEN the scenario is reported BOUND-RED (with pass/fail counts) and the process exits 1
    19	
    20	#### Scenario: SR-04 one scenario, many tests — green iff all pass
    21	- WHEN a scenario ID leads multiple test names and at least one emits `not ok`
    22	- THEN the scenario is BOUND-RED; it is BOUND-GREEN only when all its tests pass
    23	
    24	#### Scenario: SR-05 a test with no matching scenario is ORPHAN
    25	- WHEN a test name leads with an ID that no spec scenario carries
    26	- THEN it is reported ORPHAN and the process exits 1
    27	
    28	#### Scenario: SR-06 a scenario with no ID is UNIDENTIFIED
    29	- WHEN a `#### Scenario:` heading has no leading ID matching the id-pattern
    30	- THEN it is reported UNIDENTIFIED (unbindable) and the process exits 1
    31	
    32	#### Scenario: SR-07 delegates execution to the project's own test command
    33	- WHEN `--test-cmd` is given any command that emits TAP
    34	- THEN the runner runs it as-is and never assumes a language or framework (TAP is the only coupling)
    35	
    36	#### Scenario: SR-08 the id-pattern is configurable; the default recognises multi-segment and suffixed IDs
    37	- WHEN `--id-pattern` is omitted
    38	- THEN IDs are matched as `[A-Z]+(?:-[A-Z]+)*-\d+[a-z]*` — a STRICT SUPERSET of the historical `[A-Z]+-\d+` that returns the byte-identical substring for every input the old one matched, and additionally recognises multi-segment IDs (`AC-BIS-01`, `LIFE-DWS-01`) and lowercase-suffixed ones (`AC-30f`), which real projects use and which the narrow default left permanently unbindable; when given, the configured pattern governs both spec and test extraction
    39	
    40	#### Scenario: SR-09 --json emits a machine-consumable verify report
    41	- WHEN `apriori verify … --json` runs
    42	- THEN it prints valid JSON — clean, result (GREEN/GAPS/ERROR), errors, exec (status/signal/error), duplicates, specFiles, boundGreen/boundRed (with pass/fail/skip counts), unbound, orphan, unidentified — and the exit code still encodes GREEN(0)/GAPS(1)/ERROR(2)
    43	
    44	#### Scenario: SR-10 zero parsed TAP results triggers a reporter hint
    45	- WHEN `apriori verify` runs a test command that produces output but not a single parseable TAP result line
    46	- THEN the report is preceded by a warning that the test command is probably not emitting TAP (naming node's `--test-reporter=tap`), so the all-UNBOUND list is not misread as missing tests
    47	
    48	#### Scenario: SR-11 execution status is part of the verdict
    49	- WHEN the test command fails to spawn, is killed by a signal, or exits non-zero while every parsed TAP result is green
    50	- THEN verify reports the run untrustworthy and exits 2 (never GREEN); a non-zero exit with matching red TAP remains ordinary GAPS (exit 1)
    51	
    52	#### Scenario: SR-12 vacuous inputs fail closed
    53	- WHEN a spec target does not exist, or the targets contain zero spec files, or the spec files contain zero scenarios
    54	- THEN verify reports the missing input and exits 2 — an empty universe is never a GREEN
    55	
    56	#### Scenario: SR-13 spec hygiene is enforced
    57	- WHEN the same scenario ID appears in more than one scenario heading, a scenario sits inside a markdown code fence, or a test/scenario name carries an ID followed by a word character — letter, digit or underscore (`XX-01b`, `XX-01_x`)
    58	- THEN duplicates are reported with their files and force GAPS; fenced scenarios are excluded as documentation; and the suffixed ID never binds to `XX-01` — it is unidentified/untagged instead of silently truncated
    59	
    60	#### Scenario: SR-14 TAP directives and aborts never count green
    61	- WHEN a TAP result carries a `# SKIP` or `# TODO` directive, or the output contains `Bail out!`
    62	- THEN skipped results count neither pass nor fail (a scenario with only skips stays UNBOUND), and a bailout makes the run an error (exit 2) regardless of earlier green lines
    63	
    64	#### Scenario: SR-15 --test-cmd falls back to the config
    65	- WHEN `apriori verify` is invoked without `--test-cmd` and `apriori/process-config.md` carries a `test-cmd` row (written by `apriori init --test-cmd`)
    66	- THEN that command is used; with neither present, usage exits 2
